### 0B item 1 — THE `R1` RULING: **AN OUT-OF-BODY `state` IS A NORMALIZE-AND-NOTHING-MOVES DRIVE — `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` FOR EVERY VERB, AND THE CALLBACK STILL FIRES ON THE `'escape'` DRIVE**

**The as-filed contradiction, quoted from `§3.1` item 2** (kept visible there and here — it is this ruling's
evidence, not a text this pass deletes): the cell reads *"the state is normalized to `'closed'`, and every verb acts
from there"* and, **in the same sentence**, prints `('bogus','open')` ⇒ `'open'`, `changed: true` beside
`('bogus','toggle')` ⇒ `'closed'`, `changed: false`. **The two halves cannot both hold:** *"every verb acts from
there"* describes a drive evaluated **from** `'closed'` — under which a `'toggle'` acted from `'closed'` **must move
it to `'open'`** (`§2.3` item 3's `'closed'`/`'toggle'` cell) — while the printed `'toggle'` sub-row describes a
drive in which **no verb is consulted at all**. **The first half is also the half that contradicts the vendored
mechanism the cell declares.**

**THE RULING — the corrected drive-by-drive table (this is the table `§3.1` item 2's cell now carries in substance):**

| # | The drive | The returned record | The `callback` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **(1)** | out-of-body `state` × `'open'` | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | `0` |
| **(2)** | out-of-body `state` × `'close'` | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | `0` |
| **(3)** | out-of-body `state` × `'toggle'` | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | `0` |
| **(4)** | out-of-body `state` × `'escape'` | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | **exactly `1`** |
| **(5)** | out-of-body `state` × the declared no-move body (`'unknown'`) / any other unrecognized verb | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | `0` |
| **(6)** | out-of-body `state` × every other value shape (the cell's own list: omitted, `null`, `''`, `'closed '`, `'OPEN'`, a number, a boolean, `Symbol`, `12n`, an object, an array, a function, a hostile `Proxy`, a revoked `Proxy`) | `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` | `0` |

**THE EVIDENCE, three sources, each cited.**

**(a) The vendored bytes (VERIFIED-BY-READ this pass — the mechanism this unit calls, digest-pinned and never
edited, `R-4`).** `src/shared/overlay.ts` → `overlayTransition` computes `previous` as *the previous state* unless
the argument state **IS** a state body (`isStateBody(state) ? state : CLOSED`) and then computes
**`next = isStateBody(state) ? nextStateOf(previous, verb) : previous`** — **so the verb is NEVER consulted when the
argument is out-of-body**, and `changed` is derived from that `next` (`next !== previous`, i.e. `false`) for **every**
verb body. **The callback's own guard in the same function is `if (verb === VERB_ESCAPE)` — evaluated
independently of the state** — which is exactly why the `'escape'` drive is the one drive that still fires, and why
it fires with the same record as every other verb.

**(b) The foundation's contract rows, read this pass (VERIFIED-BY-READ of `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md`).**
**`§5.5.1` `P-OV-IM-1`** states it in the foundation's own words: *"an unusable or out-of-set state reads the
declared **no-move behaviour** from `'closed'` and never a fifth body"* — **no-move**, not *"acts from there"*. Its
**`§2.3` item 2's invariant** and **`§3.1` `M-3`** (*"THE NON-MOVING CELLS ANSWER THEIR OWN STATE"*) supply the
matrix half, and **`§5.5.1` `P-OV-TP-6`'s composed shape `('bogus','bogus')` ⇒ the declared normalization ⇒
`changed: false`** supplies a second composed corroboration. **The cell's first half is therefore not merely
internally contradicted; it is contradicted by the row this unit's contract rides on.**

**(c) The dossier (the STEP-0 semantics authority).** `docs/specs/pd-ui-6-adoption-dossier.md` **§1 row 3** records
the state-and-verb domain — **exactly four state bodies and no fifth, with every other state shape normalizing**,
and **the five verb bodies as the normalized alphabet with every other value normalizing to the declared no-move
verb** — and **§1 row 7** records the callback's schedule in the **verb-conditional** form: *"a callable is invoked
exactly once when, and only when, the normalized verb is `'escape'`"* — i.e. **conditional on the VERB, never on the
state's body**, which is what makes the out-of-body `'escape'` drive a firing drive. **Where a source is silent it
is said so, not filled in:** the foundation's `P-OV-IM-5` enumerates the callback's four states and does **not**
enumerate an out-of-body state; **the out-of-body callback arm is settled by the verb-only guard in the vendored
bytes (a) plus the dossier's verb-conditional row (c), and this amendment invents no arm beyond those two.**

**THE ONE HALF THAT STILL FIRES, stated because a red row will drive it.** A callable `callback` is invoked
**exactly once when, and only when, the normalized verb is `'escape'` — regardless of the state's body** — including
**from an out-of-body state** and **from `'closed'`**: on the out-of-body `'escape'` drive the record is
`{state: 'closed', changed: false}` and **the callback is still invoked exactly once** (a non-callable is never
attempted; a throw is absorbed; nothing escapes). **The corrected table above carries that column so a TestWriter
can derive the drive without re-deriving the bytes.**

**WHAT THE TESTWRITER'S ROW ASSERTS TODAY, and the ONE LIMB THE NEXT PASS OWES (recorded, so the amendment's effect
on the red set is explicit).** VERIFIED-BY-READ of `tests/pd-ui-6-modal-state-adoption.test.ts`: its `§3.1 item 2`
row asserts **only the self-consistent sub-rows** — the `'toggle'`, `'close'`, `'escape'`, `'unknown'` and
`'dismiss'` cells, each ⇒ `'closed'`, `changed: false` — and **the `('bogus','open')` cell is deliberately
UNASSERTED**, with its reason recorded in the row itself (the row's own `⟨REMAND to role_spec_writer⟩` note: *"the
`'open'` cell is NOT asserted here because the two readings disagree on it"*). **This amended clause is what makes
that cell assertable, and that is the whole point of the ruling: the next red/remand pass owes that row ONE added
limb — `('bogus','open')` ⇒ `{state: 'closed', changed: false}` — and the vendored mechanism already answers it
that way, so the limb is a drive of the ADOPTED contract, not a change to it.** **A pass that leaves the cell
unasserted after this ruling, or that asserts the superseded `'open'` reading, is a review finding against this
amendment.**

### 0B item 2 — THE `R2` RULING: **`P-MD-SM-2`'S INJECTED-DRIVE COUNT IS FOUR; "EIGHT" IS A DOCUMENTATION DEFECT AND THE ARITHMETIC IS NOT RE-DERIVED TO MATCH IT**

**The as-filed divergence, quoted (both halves kept visible at their sites):**

| The site | The as-filed reading | The corrected reading |
| --- | --- | --- |

### 0B item 4 — THE `R4` RULING: **THE DECLARED CONSUMER EDGE STAYS IN THE TREE; ITS INTERIM RED IS EXPECTED, OWNED HERE, AND CLEARED BY THE IMPLEMENTER'S LANDING**

**The as-filed mutual unsatisfiability, stated exactly (both halves kept visible at their sites).** `§6.1` ①
requires the `§3.6.1` pin re-statement to be **DISPOSED** before the red run (*"A red set authored before it is a
review finding: the pin's red would be indistinguishable from this unit's own red"*); `§3.6.1` — following the
pin's own oracle — requires that **a declared row be a REAL static edge of the tree**, with the pin's own limb
stating *"a declaration with no edge behind it is a fabricated row"* and its failure text naming the declared
consumer edge that does not exist yet. **Both cannot hold before the adapter lands.**

**What the TestWriter did, and it is VERIFIED-BY-READ this pass.** The re-statement was **performed**: the allow-list
`DECLARED_CONSUMER_EDGES` in `tests/pd-vendor-set.test.ts` now carries **exactly one added row** —
`{ file: 'src/renderer/modal-state.ts', specifier: '../shared/overlay.js', member: 'overlay', unit: 'PD-UI-6' }` —
beside the two `PD-UI-1` rows, and **both censuses and the negative control are unmoved**; and the row
**reds the pin's declared-row limb until the adapter's vendored import exists**, which is the TestWriter's reported
reading (**RECORDED READING**; measurer: the TestWriter's red-set pass). **VERIFIED-BY-READ corroboration of the
mechanism of that red:** `src/renderer/modal-state.ts` carries **no import of the vendored member at this head** (its
only matches for the module's surface are its own four declarations), so the declared edge really has no edge behind
it yet, while the allow-list really does declare it.

**THE RULING — the row STAYS in the tree, and the interim red is:**

| # | The clause |
| --- | --- |
| **1** | **EXPECTED** — it is the pin's own oracle answering truthfully at a head where the adapter has not landed |
| **2** | **OWNED BY THIS UNIT** — the declaration names its own unit (`unit: 'PD-UI-6'`) and its own spec (`§3.6.1`), and the edge it declares **lands in the same unit of work** as the declaration |
| **3** | **CLEARED by the implementer's landing of the adapter's vendored import** — `import { overlayTransition } from '../shared/overlay.js'` (`§2.1`), whose resolved path is the vendored `src/shared/overlay.ts` |
| **4** | **NOT a fabricated declaration** — a fabricated row is one that exists **only on paper** and **never** acquires an edge; this row's edge is the unit's own named landing, in the same unit of work, and the pin's message itself says so (*"the adopting unit's adapter is the edge this allow-list exists to declare"*) |
| **5** | **NOT a relaxation of the pin** — the stem-collision limb still asserts the **exact three-hit set**, the un-listed-vendored-resolving limb and its **negative control are untouched**, and the declaration is satisfied **only** by a **REAL static `from '…'` edge** (a refused evasion form still matches no pattern and still fails) |
| **6** | **NO EVASION FORM WAS TAKEN** — `await import(...)`, `require(...)`, `new URL(...)` and `pathToFileURL(...).href` stay **refused and catchable**; the declaration is for the static edge, and the adapter's own import is a static one (`§2.1`, `D-2`) |
| **7** | **DISTINGUISHABLE FROM THE UNIT'S OWN CONTRACT REDS BY ITS FAILURE TEXT** — the pin's limb names **the declared-edge oracle** (a declaration with no edge behind it); the unit's reds name **the contract surface** (the adapter's missing verb binding, its missing callback arm, its boolean-instead-of-adopted-body state word, its import census reading `0` where the adopted contract requires `1`). **That distinguishability is the property ① exists to protect, and it is preserved by this ruling rather than by a postponement.** **ONE OBSERVED CAVEAT, reported and not fixed here (this pass may not edit a test):** VERIFIED-BY-READ, the limb's current failure string hard-codes the sibling's ids (`RED (PD-UI-1 §9 item 1 / §2.1): …`), so for the `PD-UI-6` row the message's **id literal** names the sibling rather than this unit; **the message's oracle text is still the declared-edge oracle**, so the distinction holds, and the message's id literal is a TestWriter-side matter recorded here for the next pass |

**THE ALTERNATIVE, RECORDED AS CONSIDERED AND REFUSED.** The alternative — **hold the row until the adapter lands in
the same commit** — is refused, and the reasons are this amendment's, stated so the refusal is falsifiable: **(i)** a
held row does not remove the red, it **moves it into the landing commit**, where it surfaces as the pin's
**un-listed vendored-resolving limb** — the same condition **unannounced**, arising in a pass that did not author the
declaration, which is precisely the confusion ① exists to prevent; **(ii)** it would make the pin's own maintenance
route (*"one row per adopting unit"*) depend on a landing pass that need not own the pin, breaking `§3.6.1`'s
owner/same-commit discipline in the opposite direction — the declaration would arrive **after** the edge it declares
existed. **Neither reason weakens the pin:** both routes leave the pin's two limbs and its negative control exactly
as landed.

**THE OWED DISPOSITIONS THIS RULING LEAVES, named:** **(a)** the implementer's landing **must include** the adapter's
static vendored import (the edge that clears the red), **in the same commit** as the unit's other `src/**` edits;
**(b)** the pin's owning unit + the supervisor must record the clearance (the allow-list row is already landed and is
**not** re-authored); **(c)** the red-set report must show the pin's limb **still discriminating** — a synthetic
un-listed vendored-resolving edge still fails and the four refused evasion forms are still caught (`§3.6.1`'s
negative control).

**THE ESCALATIONS THIS AMENDMENT CARRIES (the ledger's closing list, so nothing travels implicit).** **(1) THE PIN'S
DISPOSITION — RE-PIN vs CARRY — IS `PENDING THE ARCHITECT'S RULING`**, with both routes' costs at `§0B` item 3 and

reds** are the supervisor's tracker-side record and every later unit's delta obligation (`§7.1`, `§7.4`, `§6.1` ③).
**(3)** **`E-6` is RESHAPED, not closed:** the pin re-statement is **performed** (VERIFIED-BY-READ) and this ruling

### 0C item 2 — THE `A-3` RULING (PROSE): **THE VENDORED GUARD IS VERB-ONLY, SO FOR A NON-CALLABLE CALLBACK AN INVOCATION IS ATTEMPTED AND ITS `TypeError` IS ABSORBED**

**The evidence, VERIFIED-BY-READ this pass (the mechanism this unit calls, digest-pinned and never edited, `R-4`).**
`src/shared/overlay.ts` → `overlayTransition`'s guard reads **`if (verb === VERB_ESCAPE) { try { (callback as () =>
void)() } catch { … } }`** — i.e. **the guard is on the VERB alone and the invocation is inside the `try`**. **A
non-callable value is therefore invoked, the engine raises a `TypeError`, and the `catch` absorbs it**; the declared
`{state, changed}` record is returned either way. **The as-filed prose at the four sites named in `A-3` described
the absent/non-callable arms as *"nothing is attempted"* / *"never attempted"* — that reading is WRONG, and it is
corrected at each site with the as-filed wording kept visible.**

**What does NOT follow, stated so no later pass over-corrects:** **NO code change and NO test change is owed.**
**(i)** The contract's **observable** clause is unchanged: **nothing throws, and the declared pair is returned** —
which is exactly what every landed assertion at those sites asserts (*"does not throw"*). **(ii)** The mechanism
still **never RETRIES** and still **never RETAINS** the callback (`§3.1` items 7/10 hold verbatim). **(iii)** The
`'escape'`-only **schedule** is unchanged (`§3.3`'s schedule clause holds verbatim) — the correction is about **what
happens to a non-callable value inside the one scheduled invocation**, not about **when** the invocation happens.
**(iv)** The corrected reading is **weaker in prose and identical in behaviour**, so **every landed row stays
green**.

### 0C item 4 — THE `A-6` RULING (the supervisor's): **THE BAN MEANS THE ATTRIBUTE IN A WRITE POSITION — NOT A TOKEN IN A TITLE, A MESSAGE, OR A NEEDLE WHOSE SUBJECT IS THE PROHIBITION**

**The contradiction, stated exactly (both halves kept visible at their sites).** `§1.4`'s DENIED table — the
clause `A-6` cites as `§1.2` (d)'s denial row — reads *"**the `inert` attribute, in any spelling, anywhere**"*; but
`§4`'s `P-MD-SM-3` limb **(e)** **requires** a token scan that carries such tokens, and
`tests/pd-ui-6-modal-state-registration`'s sibling adoption file carries **literal** `inert` in a `describe` title
and in an assertion message. **Read literally, the ban forbids the instrument the register is required to have.**

**THE RULING, in three clauses:** **(1) The ban's meaning is the ATTRIBUTE IN A WRITE POSITION** — any code that
**writes**, **removes**, **routes**, **names for a write**, or **decides the background identity of** the attribute,
in this unit's write set. **(2) It does NOT forbid the token appearing in a TEST TITLE, an ASSERTION MESSAGE, or a
SCAN NEEDLE whose subject is the prohibition itself** — a pass that must check an absence must be able to *name what
it checks*. **(3) NO DECISION OF THE PARKED ROW IS TAKEN BY THE TOKEN'S APPEARANCE**: naming the attribute in a
check is **not** naming it for a write, and `PD-OVERLAY-2`'s route / spelling / background-identity decisions remain
**that row's own gate's** (`§1.3`, `D-4`, `E-2`). **The obfuscated needle (the `EXCLUDED_NAME_NEEDLES` /
`EXCLUDED_CALL_NEEDLES` construction) may therefore be SIMPLIFIED OR KEPT, as the TestWriter prefers; NOTHING is
deleted to satisfy the ban, and no landed needle is required to change.**

### 0C item 5 — THE `A-8` RECORD (NO CHANGE OWED): **`toggle()`'s UNCONDITIONAL `onToggle` IS INHERITED-AND-KNOWN, AND IT IS THE ARCHIVED UNIT'S OWN PINNED RULE**

**The record, in three clauses.** **(1) What the adapter does:** the `toggle()` route fires **`safe(onToggle)`
unconditionally** after the transition, while **`onOpen`/`onClose` are gated on the record's `changed`** (`§2.3`
item 4's `toggle()` row — *"`onOpen`/`onClose` (per direction) **then** `onToggle`"*). **(2) Why it is
indistinguishable for the landed mechanism:** the fork's two reachable bodies make `'toggle'` **always a move**
(`'closed'`→`'open'`, `'open'`→`'closed'`), so the unconditional fire and a `changed`-gated fire **cannot be told
apart** through the adapter as the unit drives it. **(3) Why no code change is owed:** it **matches the archived
unit's own pinned rule** — the target file's `onToggle` schedule (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md`
§2.4), whose rows this unit **re-derives unchanged in substance** at `§6.3` class 1/3 — so a change here would
**contradict a re-derived pin**, and **no clause of this spec requires it**. **The exposure is recorded rather than
latent so a later pass does not read it as unexamined:** a **stub** reporting `{state: before, changed: false}` on
`'toggle'` would fire `onToggle` **with no direction notification**. **A future spec clause that requires the
direction notification to gate `onToggle` is a contract change, and it is NOT made here.**

### 0C item 6 — THE STRONGEST FALSE-GREEN (recorded verbatim in substance, because it is what `A-1` and `A-7` exist to close)

> **AN IMPLEMENTATION THAT *RECOMPUTES* THE WRITE GUARD FROM `isOpen()` INSTEAD OF READING THE ADOPTED RECORD'S
> `changed` PASSES EVERY LANDED ROW.**

**The exact exposure drive, so the claim is falsifiable rather than rhetorical.** **(i)** Inject a stub
`overlayTransition` that **reports `{state: 'open', changed: false}`** for a **no-op move**, drive the adapter's
**`close()`** against a controller whose word is **`'open'`**, and assert **(a) the frame's WRITE COUNT is `0`** and
**(b) `isOpen()` is `false`**. **A `changed`-reading implementation writes `0` and reports `false`; an
`isOpen()`-recomputing implementation writes `1` (or reports `true`) and fails.** **(ii)** The **second exposure**
is the **`close()`-route counterpart** of the same drive (`A-7`): a stub reporting **`changed: false` while the word
is `'open'`**, driven through **`close()`**, with **`isOpen() === false` and ZERO writes** asserted.

**And the two clauses that make it a FALSE-GREEN rather than a caught one: (a) THE DISCRIMINATING ASSERTION IS THE
WRITE COUNT, NOT THE CLASS** — a corpus that gets the class pair right by **computing** it (`state === 'open' ?
'is-open' : 'is-closed'`) can still produce the correct **class**, so only the **counted writes** separate the two
implementations; **(b) NO LANDED ROW TAKES THAT DRIVE THROUGH THE ADAPTER** — the mechanism-side cells
(`P-MD-SM-2`'s matrix loop) compare **values**, and the class-mirror rows that would count writes drive the
**`Mirror` reference classes**, not the adapter's wiring. **Everything else in the register is compatible with the
recomputing implementation, which is precisely why `A-1` (the register's `P-MD-SM-2` instrument), `A-7` (the coverage
hole the falsifier does not close) and `A-12` (the property text's claim) are amended together: the three close ONE
hole, not three.**

`BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS` and is escalated **in that pass**."* **And STEP 0's adoption-semantics half: an
undefined-until-answered row (or any open-semantics entry) forbids the delegable verdict and is escalated to the
architect IN THIS PASS.**

| **Verdict** | **`APPROVE-WITH-CONDITIONS`** — the dossier `§0A`'s re-verdict (2026-09-28), which **supersedes the §7 text's `BLOCKED-ON-SEMANTICS`** (kept visible there as the FILING pass's own reading). |
| **The two conditions, both in force** | **(1)** the applied inert/background-inertness half is **EXCLUDED from `PD-UI-6`'s scope by the architect's ruling** — the unit adopts the transition half and records the declaration as data; **(2)** the excluded half is **PARKED as the minted row `PD-OVERLAY-2`**, whose own gate owns the route, the attribute spelling and the background identity. |

therefore NOT do"*):** *"it may not invent an attribute name, may not pick a route, may not name the background

### 0.1 THE GATE-2 SEMANTICS COVERAGE ROW, PER ADOPTED SURFACE ELEMENT (`gate-2 rule: a missing row is a finding`)

**The gate-2 rule this filing must satisfy and demonstrate:** *every declared parameter / option / seam / callback
of the adopted surface carries its semantics row (unit / domain / referent / evaluator); a missing row is a gate-2
finding exactly as a missing return shape is.* **The dossier is the AUTHORITY for the mechanism; this filing does
not re-derive it from the bytes alone.**

| # | The adopted element | UNIT | DOMAIN | REFERENT | EVALUATOR | Its row |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **1** | `overlayTransition(state, verb, callback?)` — the pure total state-machine function | the vendored `src/shared/overlay.ts`, invoked by **this repo's adapter** | *any* `state` × *any* `verb` × *any* `callback` (optional) | the next state of a **caller-owned** overlay state word under a caller-chosen verb, plus whether the word moved — a decision **RETURNED as a record** | **the consumer (the fork)**, in its own wiring; inside the mechanism there is **no evaluator and no observer** | dossier §1 **row 1** |
| **2** | `state` (the first parameter) | the caller — **the fork's adapter is the only supplier** | *any* JavaScript value; the four declared bodies `'closed'` · `'open'` · `'held'` · `'closing'`, and **every other shape normalizes to `'closed'`** | *the caller's own word for whether an overlay is present* — an OBSERVATION about a caller-owned value | the adapter (it holds the word) | dossier §1 **row 3** + `§2.2(D)`'s `state` row |
| **3** | `verb` (the second parameter) | the caller — the fork's adapter | the five declared bodies `'open'` · `'close'` · `'toggle'` · `'escape'` · `'unknown'`; **every other value normalizes to the declared no-move body** | *what the caller asks to happen* | the mechanism normalizes it; the **adapter** chooses it | dossier §1 **row 3** |
| **4** | `callback` (the third parameter) — **OPTIONAL and explicitly NOT a seam** | the caller — **an ARGUMENT, not a fork-implemented contract** | **any** JavaScript value, and OPTIONAL; a callable is invoked **exactly once** when and only when the normalized verb is `'escape'` | *the caller's dismissal work, handed IN* — not a seam, not a listener, not a retained hook | **the mechanism invokes it; the CALLER owns its meaning.** The fork hands in a **declared no-op** (`§2.5`) | dossier §1 **row 7**; `§2.5` below |
| **5** | `OverlayTransition` + its members `state` · `changed` | the foundation (the record and both members) | `state` — the closed four-member set, never a fifth; `changed` — a `boolean` | `state` = the caller's own word after normalization, moved or not; `changed` = **the move observable**, `changed === (next !== previous)` | the fork's **class-mirror** clause consumes `changed` (`§2.4`) | dossier §1 **row 5** |
| **6** | `OverlayState` — the four-member type | the foundation | exactly four bodies, **no refusal state** | the closed vocabulary the returned `state` is always a member of | the adapter's mapping (`§2.3`, `§3.4`) | dossier §1 **row 3** |
| **7** | `overlayInertDeclaration(target, attributeName, inert)` — **recorded as DATA ONLY** | the foundation (the shape); the fork supplies the name, the identity and the boolean | *any* `target` × *any* `attributeName` × *any* `inert` | the attribute write the consumer WOULD perform, **returned as data** | **DECLARED ABSENT at this unit.** The consumer is the **minted, PARKED row `PD-OVERLAY-2`**; the absence and its reason are the ruling's. **This unit does not even CALL it** (`§1.2` (d)): it is here because the adopted module exports it and because STEP 0's question — *"does an adopted contract's parameter cross with NO referent?"* — is answered for it by the ruling | dossier §1 **row 2** (amended by `§0A`) |
| **8** | `inert` — the attribute NAME the mechanism may not own | **the CONSUMER alone** (`P-OV-7`) | the caller's own string | **NO REFERENT IN THIS UNIT** — out of scope by the ruling | **none** (nothing in this unit applies a write) | dossier §1 **row 4** |
| **9** | the fork's `installSettingsModal(host?, panels?, mounts?)` parameter shape | the fork alone | three optional untyped parameters; **the VALUES are the fork's own** | **NONE as filed — measured, not assumed.** **Disposed of by `§2.6`** (the one disposition this filing owes) | the fork's wiring | dossier §1 **row 8** |
| **10** | the `ModalControllerOptions` / `ModalController` / `createModalController` surface | **the fork alone** (fork-sourced, no foundation counterpart) | the four declared option members; the four methods | the fork's own shell-chrome state word | the fork's wiring + its tests | this filing `§2.1`/`§3.3` |
| **11** | **the seam set** | **EMPTY** | — | **`U-OVERLAY` declares no seam**; `callback` is an *argument*, and the foundation states *"This unit has no seams. That is a stated derivation, not an oversight"* (`../Provident-Electron/docs/guide/overlay.md` *What a fork must supply*) | — | dossier §1 **row 7**; proposal §4.2's `PD-UI-6` seam row |

**Coverage statement, so this block is demonstrably complete rather than asserted:** **eleven rows cover eleven
declared elements** — the two adopted value exports the fork calls (row 1; row 7 as data), **all three parameters of
the adopted function the fork calls** (rows 2/3/4), the adopted return record and both its members (row 5), the

### 1.2 THE SCOPE DECISION, stated in four clauses (this is the unit's core)

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| **(a) ADOPTED — the DECISION half** | **The vendored `src/shared/overlay.ts` → `overlayTransition(state, verb, callback?)`.** The fork's adapter obtains **the next state word and the move flag** from it, and drives its class mirror from the returned `changed`. Bytes pinned (`vendor/foundation.lock.json`'s `overlay` entry); **never edited** (`R-4`). **The fork's `createModalController` state/verb discipline is SUPERSEDED BY this function** — it is re-expressed as a call into it, not kept beside it. |
| **(b) KEPT — the wiring half** | **`installSettingsModal`**: frame/toggle/scrim/body resolution **by literal id**, the class mirror, the Escape route, click-to-close on the dedicated scrim, per-document idempotence, and fail-soft totality. **Plus the frame + dedicated scrim markup and CSS** (`src/renderer/index.html`), **unchanged by this unit**. |
| **(c) KEPT — the RE-PARENT half, explicitly, with the refusal cited** | `#panes` + `#operator-panes` → `#settings-modal-body`, **re-mount only**, no graph rebuild. **The foundation REFUSES this half** and the fork retains it deliberately (`R-2`). **This is a DIVERGENT-BUT-RETAINED behaviour and this spec says so** (`docs/specs/post-division-local-elimination-inventory.md` §2.1 `PD-OVERLAY-1`'s risk cell: *"the rebuild must record it as a divergent-but-retained behavior, not claim a clean adoption"*). |
| **(d) EXCLUDED — the APPLIED INERT-BACKGROUND WRITE** | **By the architect's ruling (`R-5`):** `overlayInertDeclaration` is adopted **AS DATA ONLY**; its fork-side consumer is **DECLARED ABSENT with its reason**; the applied half is **minted as `PD-OVERLAY-2` and PARKED**. **This unit therefore invokes nothing, reads nothing and writes nothing of the inert declaration, and names no attribute, no route and no background identity.** **`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` `§7`'s *"implement a focus trap / `inert` / top-layer … OPTIONAL / NON-goal"* clause is CARRIED UNCHANGED** — this unit does not close the focus-trap gap and must not report it closed (`P-OV-11`; `docs/specs/ui-overhaul.md` §2.1's Limits). |

**The one-clause statement of what would be a REGRESSION, so the red set can falsify it:** *if a landing leaves the
modal's open/close decision evaluated anywhere other than a call into the vendored `overlayTransition` — or if a
landing deletes the re-parent, the scrim, the Escape route or the fail-soft/idempotence guarantees while the pure
rows stay green — the adoption has either not happened or has deleted the app's shell behaviour.*

### 1.3 What this unit is NOT

Not the applied inert write (`PD-OVERLAY-2`, `§1.2` (d)) · not the focus trap / top-layer (`P-OV-11`; NON-goal
carried) · not `PD-UI-1` (`theme.ts`, W1's sibling row — **no shared cycle**, `RCA-2`/`RCA-5`) · not the divergence
harness fix (`U-DIVERGENCE-SPAWN`) · not the `index.html` markup/declaration row (`PD-UI-13`, W6a) · not the
`installShellPointers` gesture rewrite (`PD-UI-4a`) · not a tracker rewrite (the supervisor owns tracker rows) ·
**not the author of any `tests/**` byte** (the TestWriter's) · **not a foundation amendment of any kind.**

## 2. The surface (exact)

### 2.1 The fork adapter `src/renderer/modal-state.ts` — signature by signature, return shape by return shape

**Exports (the four KEPT names, plus ONE new declared type; VERIFIED-BY-READ as the current surface):**

| Export | Signature (post-adoption) | Return shape | Throws? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ModalControllerOptions` | `export interface ModalControllerOptions { initialOpen?: boolean; onOpen?: () => void; onClose?: () => void; onToggle?: () => void; callback?: unknown }` | — | — |
| `ModalController` | `export interface ModalController { open(): void; close(): void; toggle(): void; isOpen(): boolean }` | — | — |
| `ModalVerb` | **NEW** — `export type ModalVerb = 'open' \| 'close' \| 'toggle' \| 'escape'` | — | — |

finding `X-1` there) — annotate-beside, `RCA-8(c)`: THE AS-FILED CELL ABOVE IS KEPT VISIBLE AND IS SUPERSEDED. `§3.2` ITEM 7 IS THE READING THIS CELL NOW CARRIES, because the two cells CANNOT BOTH STAND.** **THE CORRECTED CELL:** ***total over malformed `VALUES`*** — `options` may be omitted, `null`, a number, a string, a `Symbol`, a function, `12n`, `{}`, a **frozen object**, an object with a non-boolean `initialOpen`, non-function notifications, or an object whose four known keys are **accessors that RETURN A VALUE**; and **a hostile accessor or a `Proxy` that THROWS ON READ is the DECLARED LIMIT of `§3.2` item 7, not a contradiction of this cell.** **MEASURED EVIDENCE (the greens artifact's row `A9`, its measurer):** a frozen options object ⇒ **no throw**; a **throwing getter at `initialOpen`** and at `onOpen` ⇒ **THREW**; a **revoked `Proxy`** ⇒ **THREW `TypeError`**; a hostile get-trap `Proxy` ⇒ **THREW** — i.e. the behaviour matches `§3.2` item 7 **exactly**. **THE CELL'S STALE CITATION IS CORRECTED TOO:** the shapes it names are governed by **`§3.2` item 7**, not by `§3.3` (the callback's seam section). **THE BEHAVIOUR GAP IS MINTED RATHER THAN SMOOTHED:** the four option reads are **not hardened** against a throwing accessor / a revoked `Proxy`, and **no landed register row covers those shapes** (`§4` `P-MD-IM-1`'s malformed-option list names no throwing getter and no revoked `Proxy`) — so the gap is filed as the defect row **`MODAL-OPTION-READ-NOT-FAIL-SOFT`** in `docs/defects.md`, **owner: the next pass that owns `src/renderer/modal-state.ts`**. **NO CODE CHANGE FOLLOWS FROM THIS CORRECTION** (`A9` is a contract-internal clause conflict; the landed rows cover a different shape set).⟩** |

**`ModalVerb` — why a declared type, and why these four bodies only.** The adapter's **own** verb vocabulary is the
four **moving** bodies of the foundation's five-body alphabet. **`'unknown'` is deliberately NOT a member of
`ModalVerb`**: in the foundation's contract it is *"the DECLARED NO-MOVE BODY"* — *"`'unknown'` in, `'unknown'`
out, and the state never moves"* (`../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.3 item 1) — so it is a
**normalization target**, not a verb the adapter may choose. **The adapter therefore emits exactly four bodies in
all four of its methods, and its `'unknown'` arm is reachable only through the mechanism's own normalization
behaviour, which this unit asserts by drive (`§4` `P-MD-IM-1` (d)).**

**The signature that is PRESERVED EXACTLY (the observable contract the 40-row file pins):**

### 2.2 `installSettingsModal` — the KEPT wiring, item by item

| # | Item | Contract (post-adoption) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | **Name, signature, visibility, position** | **`installSettingsModal(): void`**, exported, called **once** from `renderer.ts`'s boot path **after `installShellPointers(host)` and before `void host.boot(runtime).then(…)`** — the position is **unchanged** |
| 2 | **The DOM guard** | `typeof document === 'undefined' \|\| typeof document.getElementById !== 'function'` ⇒ **return, no throw** |
| 3 | **The per-document idempotence marker** | a module-level `settingsModalWiredDoc`, compared **by identity** with the current `document`; a second call on the **same** document is a **no-op**; the marker is committed at the **end** of a successful install (the existing behaviour is KEPT, and its recorded `SH7-ADV5`/`SH7-ADV6` INFO limits are carried unchanged) |
| 4 | **The element resolution** | `document.getElementById` for the **four literal ids** — `'settings-modal'` (frame), `'settings-toggle'`, `'settings-modal-scrim'`, `'settings-modal-body'`; **any one absent ⇒ return, no throw** |
| 5 | **The controller** | built by `createModalController({ initialOpen: false })` — **hidden by default** |
| 6 | **The class mirror** | `apply()` reads the frame's `className`, **filters out `is-open`/`is-closed` and keeps every other token**, pushes exactly one of the pair from the controller's state, and writes `className` once. **Called ONCE at install** (the hidden default) **and after each transition whose `changed` is `true`** |
| 7 | **The toggle affordance** | **EXACTLY ONE** `addEventListener('click', …)` on `#settings-toggle` → the controller's `toggle()` route, settled |
| 8 | **The Escape affordance** | **EXACTLY ONE** `document.addEventListener('keydown', …)` routing **the literal comparison `e.key === 'Escape'`** → the controller's **`'escape'`** route, settled; **a non-`Escape` key is ignored** |
| 9 | **The scrim affordance** | **EXACTLY ONE DIRECT** `addEventListener('click', …)` on `#settings-modal-scrim` → the controller's close route, settled. **Not document-delegated; a content click on `#settings-modal-body` never reaches it** |
| 10 | **The re-parent** | the two **existing** mounts resolved **by literal id** — `#panes`, `#operator-panes` — appended into `#settings-modal-body`, **each only when present and only when the body exposes `appendChild`**; **re-mount only** — no graph construction, no `refresh()`/`boot()`, no envelope re-run (`§2.7`) |
| 11 | **What the wiring may NOT do** | read a mount from anywhere other than the two literal ids · rebuild or boot a graph · author a pane body · hold a store, a cache or a memo · add a second class write path · add a listener beyond the three declared · install a listener for a key other than the literal `'Escape'` · add an MCP/IPC surface · **read anything from `overlayInertDeclaration`** (`§1.2` (d)) |

### 2.3 The state model, stated exactly and honestly (THE ADOPTED WORD, AND WHAT THE FORK REACHES)

**Item 1 — THE ADAPTER HOLDS THE ADOPTED FOUR-MEMBER STATE WORD.** The adapter's internal state is a value of the
adopted `OverlayState` domain (`'closed' | 'open' | 'held' | 'closing'`), initialized **from the normalized
`initialOpen`** — `true` ⇒ `'open'`, anything else ⇒ `'closed'` — and **replaced by the `state` member of the
record returned from every call.** It is **never** a bare boolean, and the adapter **never constructs a body
itself**: every body it holds came out of a returned record or out of the declared initialization.

**Item 2 — THE FOUNDATION'S DECLARED ALPHABET AND NORMALIZATION TABLE (the authority for the mechanism; dossier
§1 row 3, `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.3 item 1).** Every value the adapter may pass, and what
the mechanism does with it:

| # | The `verb` value | Normalized to | Moves? |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (1) | the string `'open'` | `'open'` | per the matrix |
| (2) | the string `'close'` | `'close'` | per the matrix |
| (3) | the string `'toggle'` | `'toggle'` | per the matrix |
| (4) | the string `'escape'` | `'escape'` | per the matrix — **and the callback is INVOKED EXACTLY ONCE, for EVERY one of the four states** |
| (5) | the string `'unknown'` | `'unknown'` | **never** — *"`'unknown'` in, `'unknown'` out, and the state never moves"* |
| (6) | **EVERY OTHER VALUE** — an unnamed string (`'dismiss'`, `'CLOSE'`, `''`, `' closed'`, `'Escape'`, `'is-open'`), the argument omitted, `null`, a number, a boolean, a `Symbol`, a `12n`, an object, an array, a function, a hostile `Proxy`, a revoked `Proxy` | **the declared no-move body** | **never** — `changed: false`, and **nothing throws** |

**The declared no-move body is `'unknown'` BY NAME, and it is a member of the alphabet, not a refusal state.** The
mechanism's only operation on a verb is an **equality test against its five declared bodies** — **no `String()`
coercion, no case fold, no trim, no prefix match.**

**Item 3 — THE FULL `4 × 5 = 20` MATRIX (every cell the foundation declares; VERIFIED-BY-READ of
`../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.3 item 2, cross-checked against `src/shared/overlay.ts`'s
`isStateBody`/`nextStateOf`).** **No cell is left `undefined-until-answered`.** `M` = moved (`changed: true`).

| current state | `'open'` ⇒ | `'close'` ⇒ | `'toggle'` ⇒ | `'escape'` ⇒ *(callback: exactly once)* | `'unknown'` ⇒ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **`'closed'`** | `'open'` · **M** | `'closed'` · — | `'open'` · **M** | `'closed'` · — | `'closed'` · — |
| **`'open'`** | `'open'` · — | `'closed'` · **M** | `'closed'` · **M** | `'closed'` · **M** | `'open'` · — |
| **`'held'`** | `'held'` · — | `'closed'` · **M** | `'held'` · — | `'closed'` · **M** | `'held'` · — |
| **`'closing'`** | `'open'` · **M** | `'closed'` · **M** | `'open'` · **M** | `'closed'` · **M** | `'closing'` · — |

**The four states' semantics, so no cell reads as arbitrary:** `'closed'` = no overlay present · `'open'` =
present · `'held'` = present with the caller's hold condition in force (**so `'toggle'` and `'open'` do NOT move
it**) · `'closing'` = present with its close in flight (**so `'open'` and `'toggle'` RE-OPEN it**).

**The three invariants carried**, each asserted by this unit's register: **(i)** `changed === (next !== previous)`
on **every** cell and every out-of-alphabet drive (`§4` `P-MD-TP-1`); **(ii)** **every NON-MOVING cell answers ITS
OWN state**, and `changed` is `false` for exactly those cells; **(iii)** **no state outside the four bodies ever
appears in a returned record.**

**Item 4 — THE ADAPTER'S OWN MAPPING: ITS FOUR METHODS TO THE VERBS IT USES.** **The adapter emits EXACTLY FOUR
verb bodies and exactly one `state` argument form. `'unknown'` is never emitted; `'escape'` is emitted only by the
close route.**

| The method the call site/test uses | The verb the adapter binds | The record it consumes | State after | `changed` | Effect |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `open()` | **`'open'`** | `{state, changed}` | `'open'` | *from the matrix* | `onOpen` fires **only when `changed`** |
| `close()` | **`'escape'`** | `{state, changed}` | `'closed'` | *from the matrix* | `onClose` fires **only when `changed`**; **the `callback` is invoked exactly once** (`§2.5`) |
| `toggle()` | **`'toggle'`** | `{state, changed}` | *from the matrix* | **always `true` from both reachable bodies** | `onOpen`/`onClose` (per direction) **then** `onToggle` |
| `isOpen()` | **none — a READ; no call** | — | unchanged | — | returns the `state` member's mapping: `'open'` ⇒ `true`; **every other body ⇒ `false`** |

**Item 5 — THE REACHABLE SET: TWO, AND THE OTHER TWO ARE ADOPTED VOCABULARY THE FORK NEVER ENTERS.**
**Proved from the matrix, not asserted:** starting at `'closed'`, **every cell of the reachable closure under
`{'open', 'escape', 'toggle'}` lands in `{'closed', 'open'}`** — `'closed'`+`'open'`→`'open'`; `'open'`+`'escape'`→
`'closed'`; `'open'`+`'toggle'`→`'closed'`; `'closed'`+`'toggle'`→`'open'`. **`'held'` is reachable only from
`'held'`, and `'closing'` only from `'closing'`.** **Consequence, stated in three clauses so it cannot be
misread:**

1. **It is NOT a narrowing of the module's closed set.** The adapter's state word **is** the four-member
   `OverlayState`; two of its four members are simply **not reachable from the fork's own four verbs**. **`P-OV-1`/
   `P-OV-3` place the state word with the CALLER, and the caller's reachable subset is the caller's business** —
   the mechanism decides no policy and has no opinion about which bodies a caller uses.
2. **It is NOT a licence to emulate them.** A landing that **fabricates** `'held'` or `'closing'` — by writing the
   word directly, by a second state authority, or by passing a body the mechanism did not return — is **a review
   finding**: the word is an **observation** taken from a returned record, never a value the adapter invents
   (`../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.3 item 4: *"the caller owns the state; this mechanism OBSERVES
   a state word and REPORTS the next one"*).
3. **It is NOT a policy question.** The fork's modal has no hold condition and no close-in-flight phase, and the
   `W-U-SHELL-7-Q2` ruling pins the open state as **session-only transient shell state**. **No row may require the
   fork to enter `'held'`/`'closing'`, and no row may forbid the adopted vocabulary from carrying them.**

### 2.4 THE `changed`-driven CLASS MIRROR — the adoption's equivalence to the landed `settle` guard

**The clause:** **the frame's class is written exactly when the adopted record reports that the state actually
changed.** Formally, per affordance call:

1. **The adapter takes `before` = its held state word.**
2. **It calls `overlayTransition(before, verb, callback?)`** and **replaces its word** with the returned `state`.
3. **It reads the returned `changed` member** — `changed === (state !== before)` by the foundation's own identity
   (`../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.1 item 2) — and **performs the class write if, and only if, it
   is `true`.**
4. **An idempotent Escape or scrim no-op (`changed === false`) performs NO class write.**

**How `changed` is read — pinned, so a red row can falsify it:** **as the returned record's own member, by value**;
**`changed` is never recomputed from `isOpen()`, never inferred from the verb's identity, and never taken from a
pre-call snapshot comparison.** The landed guard was `controller.isOpen() !== before`; the adopted guard is the
**record's** own move observable, and **that is a strengthening, not a substitution**: the foundation states
`changed` is *"an OBSERVABLE, never the caller's claim, and never the verb's identity"*.

**Why this is the equivalence the adoption claims, and how a red row falsifies it (`§4` `P-MD-SM-2`):**
`isOpen()` maps `state === 'open'`, so `isOpen() !== before-isOpen` and `changed` **agree on every cell of the
`4 × 5` matrix** — including the two reachable ones and **including the `'held'`/`'closing'` cells the fork never
enters** (an assertion the class mirror's equivalence can make **only** because the guard moved to the adopted
record). **The discriminating control:** a corpus that reads `isOpen()` before and after (the landed guard),
**and** a corpus that treats any non-`'open'` returned body as a change, **must both fail** the row — the second
because it class-writes on the `'held'`/`'closing'` arms, and the first because it cannot be driven at all
**without bypassing the record**.

**The class write itself is unchanged, and its three properties are KEPT exactly:**

| Property | Contract | Pinned by |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **the XOR pair** | the frame's tokens carry **exactly one** of `is-open`/`is-closed`, from the state word | `state 8`/`state 9`/`state 10`/`state 11`/`state 14` |
| **sibling preservation** | every **non-`is-*`** token on the frame survives every write | `SH7-ADV3` |
| **one write per real transition, none on an idempotent no-op** | driven by `changed` (`§2.4` clauses 3/4) | `state 13`, `SH7-ADV2`, `F3`, `F4` |

### 2.5 **THE ESCAPE CONTRACT — THE RULING THIS FILING OWES** (with its justification and its test consequence)

**The two readings that are both pinned somewhere, stated before the ruling (so the ruling is not read as
overlooking either):**

| # | The reading | Where it is pinned |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **①** | **The foundation invokes the `callback` EXACTLY ONCE on the normalized verb `'escape'`, for EVERY one of the four states, `'closed'` included** — and from `'closed'` it returns **`changed: false`**. *"The falsifier: a module that … skips it on `'escape'` from `'closed'` … FAILS `P-OV-IM-5`."* | `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md` §2.3 item 1 row **(4)** + §2.3 item 2's `'escape'` column; `P-OV-IM-5`; `§3.2 F-3` |
| **②** | **The fork's landed `close()` on an already-closed modal is an idempotent no-op with NO class write**, and the **Escape** route reaches it through the `settle` guard. | `tests/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.test.ts`'s **`SH7-ADV2`** (*"Escape on an already-closed modal must NOT write the class"*), **`state 13`**, **`F3`**; `docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §2.4 (*"`Escape` when the modal is already closed → `controller.close()` is a no-op (the controller's idempotency absorbs it; no class write, no `onClose`)"*) + §4 `F3` |

**THE RULING — this unit's contract is the union of the two, because they are compatible, and the binding is what
reconciles them:**

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| **1** | **THE ESCAPE ROUTE BINDS THE VERB `'escape'` — not `'close'`.** The adapter's close route (`close()`, reached by **both** the Escape affordance and the scrim-click affordance) calls `overlayTransition(currentState, 'escape', callback?)`. **Reason: the foundation declares the `'escape'` column's callback obligation by name and states the falsifier explicitly; the fork's Escape route is exactly the `Escape`-equivalent the foundation's design intends to receive, and `'close'` deliberately carries no callback obligation.** |
| **2** | **THE VERB CHOICE IS OBSERVABLY FREE — VERIFIED, NOT ASSUMED.** **`'close'` and `'escape'` produce the SAME state and the SAME `changed` in all four rows** of the matrix (`'closed'`/`'closed'`/`'closed'`/`'closed'`; `changed` false/true/true/true) — VERIFIED-BY-READ of the matrix's `'close'` and `'escape'` columns. **So the ruling changes no state, no change flag, no class write and no wiring order.** |
| **3** | **WHAT THE CALLBACK IS: a caller-supplied ARGUMENT, defaulting to a DECLARED NO-OP, and the fork SUPPLIES NOTHING on every landed route.** `ModalControllerOptions` gains **one optional member `callback?: unknown`**; the adapter passes `options.callback` straight into `overlayTransition`'s third slot. **The wiring passes none** (it calls `createModalController({ initialOpen: false })`), so **on every landed route the argument is absent and the foundation's own "absent/non-callable ⇒ the declared pair is returned and nothing throws" arm is what runs.** **No dismissal work is moved into it — see clause 5.** |
| **4** | **WHAT FIRES AND WHAT DOES NOT WHEN ALREADY CLOSED — the honest, clause-by-clause answer.** Escape (or a scrim click) while the frame is already closed: **(i)** `overlayTransition('closed', 'escape', cb)` is called; **(ii)** **the `callback` IS invoked exactly once** (an absent callback is a non-callable: **an invocation is ATTEMPTED and its `TypeError` is ABSORBED by the mechanism's own `try`/`catch`, unobservable to the caller** — **⟨CORRECTED 2026-09-29 (finding `A-3`; the ruling and its evidence are at `§0C` item 2) — annotate-beside, as-filed wording kept: this clause AS FILED read *"an absent callback is a non-callable: nothing is attempted, and nothing throws"*. THE VENDORED BYTES GUARD THE CALLBACK BY VERB ONLY** (`if (verb === VERB_ESCAPE) { try { callback() } catch {} }`, VERIFIED-BY-READ of `src/shared/overlay.ts` → `overlayTransition`), **so for a NON-CALLABLE value the invocation IS attempted and its `TypeError` is absorbed** — **the observable clause (nothing throws, the declared pair is returned) is UNCHANGED, no code change and no test change is owed, and every landed assertion at this site is a *"does not throw"* assertion.**⟩); **(iii)** the returned record is `{ state: 'closed', changed: false }`; **(iv)** **the frame's class is NOT written** — `changed` is `false`; **(v)** **no `onClose` fires** — `onClose` is gated on `changed` (`§2.3` item 4); **(vi)** **no state changes.** |
| **5** | **WHY THE NO-OP AND NOT THE REAL DISMISSAL WORK.** The dismissal work this modal has is **already done before the call**: the class write is the **caller's**, driven by `changed` (`§2.4`), and the fork authors **no other dismissal effect** (no focus restore, no top-layer change, no store write — `docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-modal-state.md` `§2.7` and `W-U-SHELL-7-Q3`). **Handing in anything with a side effect would create a SECOND write authority and would break the single-managed-write discipline** (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §2.3: *"there is no independent 'open'/'close' DOM flip"*). **The fork therefore declares the callback a NO-OP and makes the invocation schedule observable by construction rather than by effect** — which is exactly what the register's callback row drives (`§4` `P-MD-IM-2`). |
| **6** | **THE OBSERVABLE 40-ROW BEHAVIOUR IS PRESERVED — VERIFIED CLAUSE BY CLAUSE.** `SH7-ADV2`'s counted-writes assertion (exactly one write at install, +1 per real transition, **+0** on Escape/scrim while closed) holds because `changed` is `false` there; `state 13`'s *"Escape while closed = no-op"* holds for the same reason; `F3`/`F4`'s *"no class churn"* holds; `F8`'s *"a non-`Escape` key never closes, never throws"* is **unchanged** (the `e.key === 'Escape'` literal comparison stays in the wiring, and a non-`Escape` key never reaches the mechanism at all); `state 10`'s *"Escape closes while open"* holds (`changed: true`). **Nothing observable moves.** |
| **7** | **THE COST OF THE RULING, stated rather than hidden.** The fork now **carries** an invocation whose effect it declares empty. **The benefit is the whole of the adoption's fidelity on this half**: the fork no longer merely *coincides* with the foundation's callback clause — it **satisfies** it, and the satisfaction is **observable in a node row** (a recording callback passed through `ModalControllerOptions` sees count `1` on close-from-any-state and count `0` on every other verb). **Under the rejected alternative** (bind `'close'`), the callback argument would be a **declared parameter of the adopted surface with no reference at all** — the exact gate-2 defect this filing exists to avoid. |
| **8** | **THE REJECTED ALTERNATIVES, each with its reason.** **(a) Bind `'close'`:** rejected — it would make `callback` a **dead declared parameter** (a gate-2 finding by the gate-2 rule this spec's `§0` satisfies) and would leave the `'escape'` column of the adopted matrix **undriven by any consumer in `src/**`**. **(b) Route the Escape affordance to the mechanism directly, outside the controller:** rejected — it would give the wiring a **second state authority** (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §2.3's single-source-of-truth clause) and would leave `close()` bound elsewhere. **(c) Emulate the foundation's `'escape'`-from-`'closed'` obligation by firing the callback *in the wiring*:** rejected — the mechanism **invokes** it; a wiring-side emulation would be a claim about the mechanism made outside it, and would not be the adopted surface. |

**THE ROWS THAT PIN THE RULING (name by name, so the ruling is falsifiable and not merely stated):**

| The row | What it pins |
| --- | --- |
| `SH7-ADV2` (re-derived, `§6.3` class 3) | the counted class writes: **exactly one at install**, `+1` per real transition, **`+0` on Escape and on a scrim click while already closed** |
| `state 13` (re-derived) | *"exactly ONE frame class write per transition (open/close), none on idempotent no-ops"* |
| `F3` · `F4` (re-derived) | *"Escape while closed is an idempotent no-op (no class write)"* · *"scrim-click while closed is an idempotent no-op (no class write)"* |
| `state 10` (re-derived) | *"Escape closes: a document keydown Escape while open flips the frame to `.is-closed`"* |
| `F8` (re-derived) | a non-`Escape` key is ignored — **never closes, never throws** |
| **`P-MD-IM-2` (NEW, `§4`)** | **the callback arm: a recording callback through `ModalControllerOptions` sees exactly `1` invocation on the close route from EVERY state (including from `'closed'`), `0` on `open()`/`toggle()`, and `0` when the verb normalizes to the no-move body; a throwing callback is absorbed and the declared pair is still returned; a non-callable is never attempted** — **⟨CORRECTED 2026-09-29 (finding `A-3`; the ruling and its evidence are at `§0C` item 2) — annotate-beside, as-filed wording kept: *"a non-callable is never attempted"* is WRONG. THE HONEST READING: for a NON-CALLABLE callback AN INVOCATION IS ATTEMPTED AND ITS `TypeError` IS ABSORBED — unobservable to the caller** (the guard is on the VERB alone: `if (verb === VERB_ESCAPE) { try { callback() } catch {} }`, VERIFIED-BY-READ of `src/shared/overlay.ts`). **No code change, no test change and no term move follows: every landed assertion here is a *"does not throw"* assertion.**⟩ |
| **`P-MD-SM-2` (NEW, `§4`)** | **the equivalence: the `changed`-driven mirror and the landed `isOpen()`-comparison guard agree on every reachable transition and on every `4 × 5` cell** |
| **`P-MD-IM-1` (NEW, `§4`)** | **the totality arm: an `'escape'` close from an already-closed frame leaves the state at `'closed'`, `changed: false`, and the class untouched** |

### 2.6 **THE `installSettingsModal` PARAMETER DISPOSITION** (dossier row 8 — the one disposition this filing owes)

**The finding, restated from the dossier's row 8 (which is the authority):** the fork's
`installSettingsModal(host?: unknown, panels?: unknown, mounts?: unknown)` accepts **three optional untyped
parameters whose values are never read** — *"the body resolves every element by literal id through
`document.getElementById` and **reads no member of any argument**"*. The dossier records its `REFERENT` as **`NONE`
— measured, not assumed** — and states that the spec **owes this exactly one of two dispositions**.

**THE RULING — OPTION (B): THE PARAMETERS ARE DROPPED FROM THE PINNED SURFACE, AND THE ONE CALL SITE IS
RE-POINTED IN THE SAME COMMIT.**

**The clauses, with their reasons in order of binding force:**

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| **1** | **The new signature is `installSettingsModal(): void`.** The three parameters, their types, their optionality and their JSDoc *"accepted for signature shape only"* clause are **removed**, together with the `void`-style shape obligation they carried. |
| **2** | **Why NOT option (A) — "make the wiring consume them".** **(i)** The only thing the third argument currently carries is the **record *of the same two literal ids the body already hard-codes*** — `{ panes: 'panes', operatorPanes: 'operator-panes' }` — so consuming it would replace two literal ids with **two literal keys read off an untyped argument**, i.e. it would **relocate the constants and add a shape-validation branch for no referent gained**. **(ii)** `host` and `panels` are **not mount carriers at all**: `host` is a `SidebarPanes` instance and `panels` a **nullable** `SecurePanels | null` (**VERIFIED-BY-READ of the call site and of the test file's `install({}, null, …)` calls**), and the modal's contract forbids it from **calling into either** — *"The wiring does NOT construct/re-construct either graph, does NOT call `refresh()`/`refreshDebug()`/`boot()`, does NOT author any pane body"* (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §2.5). **Consuming them would therefore mean calling the graphs the unit is forbidden to touch — a contract violation dressed as a fix.** **(iii)** A half-measure — consume `mounts` and keep `host`/`panels` — leaves **two of the three parameters still no-referent**, i.e. it does not discharge the finding; and it makes the disposition read as *"mostly consumed"*, which is worse than a clean statement. |
| **3** | **The cost, stated in full (the pin consequence).** **The parameters are a PINNED INPUT of `docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §2.6** — *"Signature (free shape, pinned inputs): `installSettingsModal(host?, panels?, mounts?)` where `host` is the `SidebarPanes`, `panels` is the `SecurePanels \| null`, and `mounts` is a small object of the two mount element ids/elements"* — and the target test file invokes the wiring through that shape at its `loadModalState`/`requireExport` helper and in every `§3b`/`§4` row (`install({}, null, { panes: 'panes', operatorPanes: 'operator-panes' })`). **Dropping the parameters therefore REDS those call shapes, and the re-derivation (`§6.3` class 3) is where they are re-pointed.** **`§2.6`'s clause is amended by this filing's ruling; the file itself is NOT edited by this pass** (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` is not in this pass's write set), **and the correction is recorded here so the two documents are not in contradiction when the landing pass reads them.** |
| **4** | **What is NOT touched by the disposition.** *(a)* **the two literal ids stay** — `#panes` and `#operator-panes` are still resolved by `document.getElementById` **with those literal spellings**, because they are the **authored markup's own ids** (`src/renderer/index.html`), pinned by `docs/decisions.md` `DECIDED: MODAL-SETTINGS-REPARENT` and asserted by `tests/unit-u-shell-1-w2n4-operator-grid-area.test.ts` / `tests/sidebar-panes.test.ts`. **The adoption must not make the mount identity a caller-supplied string**: the mechanism owns no element name (`P-OV-7`/`P-OV-2`), and the fork's ids are **markup**, not policy. *(b)* **the re-parent behaviour is unchanged** (`§2.7`) — this disposition changes **resolution plumbing only**, never **which** elements move or **when**. *(c)* **`renderer.ts`'s call ORDER is unchanged** — after `installShellPointers(host)`, before `void host.boot(runtime).then(…)`. |
| **5** | **THE `§2.8` SOURCE-PIN CONSEQUENCE, named so it is a re-statement and not a surprise.** The target file's re-parent pin asserts the **literal call text** `getElementById('panes')` and `getElementById('operator-panes')`. **Those literals SURVIVE this disposition** (clause 4(a)) **because the parameters were dropped rather than consumed** — so the pin is **re-derived for the new invocation shape only** (the helper's `install(...)` becomes a no-arg call), **not for its own subject.** **That is one of the reasons option (B) was chosen over option (A)**: option (A) would have moved the ids behind an argument and reddened that pin's *subject*, buying a shape-validation branch and losing a literal-id assertion. |
| **6** | **The escalation this ruling does NOT take.** Whether `docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` `§2.6` should be **amended in place** (an annotate-beside dated correction, the repo's convention) or **superseded by a new clause** is **the supervisor's / owning spec's call** — the unit is `GREEN / COMPLETE` and adjudicated, so a spec amendment is a recorded act, not this filing's. **`§9` item 5 carries it.** |

### 2.7 The KEPT re-parent half — behaviour preserved EXACTLY

| # | Clause |
| --- | --- |
| **1** | **Both EXISTING mounts move**: `#panes` (the SecurePanels isolated graph's mount) and `#operator-panes` (the SidebarPanes host's operator graph's mount) are appended into `#settings-modal-body`. **`modalBody.children.length === 2` after install, with both mounts present and `parent` set to the body.** |
| **2** | **RE-MOUNT ONLY**: no graph construction, no `refresh()`/`refreshDebug()`/`boot()`, no pane body authored, no `buildOperatorEnvelope` re-run. **The DomAdapter keeps its mount ownership regardless of ancestry** — *"neither graph is re-created"* (`docs/decisions.md` `DECIDED: MODAL-SETTINGS-REPARENT`, whose text this clause quotes). |
| **3** | **The re-parent runs while the modal is CLOSED (hidden) as well as when open** — ancestry-only, no destroy; isolation and MCP-invisibility are preserved (`docs/specs/unit-u-shell-7-settings-modal.md` §4 `F10`). |
| **4** | **THE HARD INVARIANT: `#app` / the stage / the app-graph pane frames / `#tab-strip` / the gutters NEVER enter the modal.** The modal's children are **exactly** the two operator mounts. |
| **5** | **Each mount is appended only when it EXISTS and only when the body exposes `appendChild`** — an absent mount is skipped, never fabricated. |
| **6** | **THE REFUSAL THIS HALF RETAINS AGAINST, CITED (R-2):** `../Provident-Electron/docs/specs/overlay.md`'s **title** (*"the pure total overlay state machine + the INERT-BACKGROUND DECLARATION (returned as data) — **with the RE-PARENT HALF REFUSED**"*), its **`§1` item 3** (the three clauses with reasons: the mechanism holds **NO NODE** and moves nothing; **a RETURNED MOVE PLAN is a move with NO OBSERVABLE ON ANY LAYER THIS REPO OWNS**; and the mechanism **performing** the move *"would additionally require the shim's node-move bookkeeping to be owned here, which it is not"*), its **`§2.5` item 6** (*"a refusal that is stated is an honest narrowing; a refusal that is silent is a charter violation"*), and the checkable bans **`P-OV-10`** (*"NO MOVE, NO PARENT, NO PORTAL, AND NO RETURNED MOVE PLAN"*) and **`R-12`** (the refused-mutation row). |
| **7** | **THE PIN (`docs/decisions.md` `DECIDED: MODAL-SETTINGS-REPARENT`, ACTIVE) and the divergent-but-retained record**: the fork's retention is a **deliberate divergence**, recorded in the implementation, in the divergence table and in a `decisions.md` pin (`docs/FORK-DIVERGENCE.md` §3 rule 4's three places) — and **already landed**. **A pass that files this adoption as clean is filing a false claim** (`docs/specs/post-division-local-elimination-inventory.md` §2.1 `PD-OVERLAY-1`'s risk cell). |

### 2.8 What this unit ADOPTS as a prohibition (carried, never relaxed)

The vendored module's prohibitions **bind its bytes, which this unit does not touch**, and they bind **the fork's
adapter in the direction that matters**: the adapter may **not** push the fork's vocabulary or its DOM into the
mechanism. Concretely: the adapter **never** passes a token it owns (`is-open`, `is-closed`, `settings-modal`,
`scrim`) as a **verb** or a **state** argument in a way the mechanism would have to interpret; it **never** asks the
mechanism to resolve, place, style or watch an element (`P-OV-2`/`P-OV-8`/`P-OV-9`/`R-2`/`R-10`); it **never** asks
it to hold state or retain the callback (`P-OV-3`, `I-4`, `P-OV-IM-5`'s no-retention limb); and it **never** imports
a sibling into it (`R-4`/`S-OV-9`). **Every one of these already holds for the vendored bytes and is RE-ASSERTED
by the register (`§4` `P-MD-SM-3`) so the adoption cannot silently reverse it.** **The dossier's two binding
constraints, carried verbatim in substance (`C-2`/`C-3`):** **(i)** the fork **may not move the key route into the
mechanism**, and **may not ask the mechanism to watch a key**; **(ii)** the fork **may not ask the mechanism to
resolve, place or style an element.** **Neither prohibition is an exemption, and this filing claims no exemption
(`C-3`: *"THIS IS NOT AN EXEMPTION AND MUST NOT BE READ AS ONE"*).**

**Read from the vendored bytes and from the foundation's contract this pass (VERIFIED-BY-READ of
`src/shared/overlay.ts`, 62 lines, zero imports; and of the dossier's §1 rows 1/3/5/7, which are the authority for
the mechanism). The red set must derive from these rows, not from a re-derivation of the bytes alone.**

| # | Input shape | Declared outcome | Throws? |
